I must use specific severity labels (`Minor`/`Major`) and output a strict JSON format.

2.  **Analyze the Source (BP):**
    *   *Source:* "Mesmo que sua equipe não esteja sempre ganhando, ou esteja perdendo, existe um benefício real através da experiência."
    *   *Meaning:* Even if your team is not always winning, or is losing, there is a real benefit through the experience.

3.  **Analyze the Candidate (EP):**
    *   *Candidate:* "Mesmo que a tua equipa não esteja sempre a ganhar, ou esteja a perder, existe um benefício real através da experiência."

4.  **Comparison and Error Check:**

    *   **"Mesmo que"**: Matches. (Meaning preservation, Grammaticality)
    *   **"sua" (BP) vs. "a tua" (EP)**: This is a shift from formal/standard BP pronoun usage ("sua") to informal/familiar EP pronoun usage ("tua").
        *   *Criterion 3 (Conservative rewriting):* Is this change unnecessary? The source uses "sua" (which can be formal or standard). The candidate uses "tua" (informal). Since the context (a general essay/advice) doesn't strictly demand informality, changing the register unnecessarily constitutes a violation of conservative rewriting, especially if the original register was neutral/standard.
        *   *Assessment:* This is a stylistic/register choice, but since the source used "sua," changing it to the distinctly informal "tua" is an unnecessary deviation from the source's register.
        *   *Error Identification:* "a tua" (or just "tua" if we focus on the possessive adjective). Let's focus on the possessive adjective change.
    *   **"equipe" (BP) vs. "equipa" (EP)**: Standard EP spelling/lexicon. Correct adaptation.
    *   **"não esteja sempre ganhando" (BP) vs. "não esteja sempre a ganhar" (EP)**: This is a necessary adaptation from the BP gerund construction ("ganhando") to the EP periphrastic construction ("a ganhar"). This is a correct adaptation, not an error.
    *   **"ou esteja perdendo" (BP) vs. "ou esteja a perder" (EP)**: Necessary adaptation (perdendo -> a perder). Correct adaptation.
    *   **"existe um benefício real através da experiência"**: Matches perfectly.

5.  **Refining the Error:**
    *   The only potential error is the shift from "sua" to "a tua."
    *   The change from "sua" (standard/neutral) to "a tua" (informal) is an unnecessary change in register/formality, violating the conservative rewriting principle.
    *   *Severity:* Since the meaning is preserved and the sentence is grammatically correct EP, but the change is unnecessary and deviates from the source's register, it is a `Minor` error.

6.  **Final JSON Construction:**

Exact raw continuation
thought
The user wants me to assign a holistic quality score (0-100) to an EP candidate translation based on provided context, source sentence, and validated error annotations.

**Source Sentence (BP):**
Mesmo que sua equipe não esteja sempre ganhando, ou esteja perdendo, existe um benefício real através da experiência.

**Candidate (EP):**
Mesmo que a tua equipa não esteja sempre a ganhar, ou esteja a perder, existe um benefício real através da experiência.

**Validated Errors:**
`{"errors":[{"text":"a tua","severity":"Minor"}]}`

**Evaluation Criteria:**
1. Meaning preservation.
2. Required BP→EP adaptation.
3. Conservative rewriting.
4. EP grammaticality, naturalness, context, and register.

**Analysis:**

1. **Meaning Preservation:** The meaning is perfectly preserved.
2. **BP→EP Adaptation:**
    * BP: "sua equipe" -> EP: "a tua equipa" (Change in pronoun/possessive form, which is a valid adaptation, though "a sua equipa" might also be used depending on register).
    * BP: "ganhando" / "perdendo" (Gerund form) -> EP: "a ganhar" / "a perder" (Infinitive structure with 'a', common in EP). This is a correct adaptation.
3. **Conservative Rewriting:** The structure is very close to the original.
4. **Grammaticality/Naturalness/Register:**
    * The use of "a tua" (informal/familiar 'tu' form) instead of "a sua" (formal/standard 'você' or 'sua') is flagged as a minor error. While "a tua" is grammatically correct in EP, if the context implies a standard or slightly formal register (which is often the case in general motivational/educational texts), the shift to 'tu' might be considered a register mismatch or a stylistic choice that deviates from the most neutral translation.
    * The rest of the sentence ("Mesmo que...", "não esteja sempre", "existe um benefício real através da experiência") is perfectly natural and correct EP.

**Scoring:**

The error is minor ("a tua"). It affects the register/formality slightly, but the sentence is otherwise excellent, highly natural, and perfectly conveys the meaning.

*   If the register was strictly formal, "a tua" would be a noticeable deviation.
*   If the register is conversational/semi-informal, "a tua" is fine.

Given that the error is minor and the overall quality is high (excellent flow, correct grammar, perfect meaning), the score should be high, but not 100 because an error exists.

*   85–100: Very good
*   65–80: Good

I lean towards the upper end of "Good" or the lower end of "Very good." Since the error is minor and doesn't impede understanding or flow, I will assign a high score.

Let's go with 85. It reflects the high quality while penalizing the minor register/formality deviation identified in the annotation.```json
{
"score": 85

2.  **Analyze the Source (BP):**
    *   *Source:* "Mesmo que sua equipe não esteja sempre ganhando, ou esteja perdendo, existe um benefício real através da experiência."
    *   *Meaning:* Even if your team is not always winning, or is losing, there is a real benefit through the experience.

3.  **Analyze the Candidate (EP):**
    *   *Candidate:* "Mesmo que a tua equipa não esteja sempre a ganhar ou a perder, existe um benefício real através da experiência."

4.  **Comparison and Error Check:**

    *   **Meaning Preservation:**
        *   BP: "sua equipe" (your team - formal/standard)
        *   EP: "a tua equipa" (your team - informal/familiar)
        *   *Check:* The meaning is preserved. The shift from *sua* (formal/standard) to *tua* (informal/familiar) is a register change, but not a meaning change.
        *   BP: "ganhando" (gerund)
        *   EP: "a ganhar" (periphrastic construction)
        *   *Check:* Both convey the continuous action of winning/losing. This is a necessary/acceptable adaptation (BP gerund vs. EP periphrasis).
        *   *Conclusion:* Meaning is preserved.

    *   **Required Variety Adaptation (BP -> EP):**
        *   The shift from *sua* to *tua* is a register choice. Since the original BP used *sua* (which is acceptable in EP too, but *tua* is also common), this is not a *required* adaptation, but a stylistic choice.
        *   The use of the periphrastic construction ("a ganhar/a perder") is a standard and correct EP adaptation for the BP gerund ("ganhando/perdendo").

    *   **Conservative Rewriting:**
        *   The change from *sua* to *tua* is a change in register, which might be considered unnecessary if the goal was strictly conservative rewriting, but it is grammatically correct EP.

    *   **EP Grammaticality and Naturalness:**
        *   "Mesmo que a tua equipa não esteja sempre a ganhar ou a perder..." - Grammatically correct.
        *   "...existe um benefício real através da experiência." - Grammatically correct.

5.  **Re-evaluation of Register/Style:**
    *   The source uses *sua* (standard/formal address).
    *   The candidate uses *tua* (informal address).
    *   If the context (a general advice passage) implies a standard, slightly formal tone (as often found in instructional/expository texts), the shift to *tua* is a register mismatch, making it an unnecessary change (violating the conservative rewriting principle if we assume the original register should be maintained).
    *   However, the prompt states: "Do not penalize a BP expression that is also acceptable in EP, and do not penalize a correct necessary BP→EP adaptation." Both *sua* and *tua* are acceptable in EP.

Since the sentence is perfectly grammatical and natural in EP, I must determine if this stylistic choice constitutes an error under the given constraints.

    *   *Self-Correction/Refinement:* The criteria focus on *genuine errors*. A change in register (sua -> tua) is often flagged as an error in conservative rewriting if the original register is assumed, but if the resulting sentence is perfectly natural EP, it might pass. Given the high standard of the candidate, I conclude there are no *genuine* errors that meet the criteria for a `Minor` or `Major` penalty.

I must use specific severity labels (`Minor`/`Major`) and output a strict JSON format.

2.  **Analyze the Source (BP):**
    *   *BP Source:* "E, sem uma parada sequer, nem baldeações demoradas, você está sentado em um ônibus que vai para o seu bairro."
    *   *Meaning:* And, without a single stop, nor lengthy transfers, you are sitting in a bus that goes to your neighborhood.
    *   *Key elements:*
        *   "E" (And)
        *   "sem uma parada sequer" (without a single stop)
        *   "nem baldeações demoradas" (nor lengthy transfers)
        *   "você está sentado" (you are sitting)
        *   "em um ônibus" (in a bus)
        *   "que vai para o seu bairro" (that goes to your neighborhood)

3.  **Analyze the Candidate (EP):**
    *   *EP Candidate:* "E, sem uma paragem sequer, nem baldeações demoradas, estás sentado num autocarro que vai para o teu bairro."

4.  **Perform Detailed Comparison and Error Check:**

    *   **"E, sem uma paragem sequer"**:
        *   BP: "parada" (stop)
        *   EP: "paragem" (stop)
        *   *Check:* This is a standard, acceptable adaptation (BP *parada* -> EP *paragem*). No error.

    *   **"nem baldeações demoradas"**:
        *   BP: "nem baldeações demoradas"
        *   EP: "nem baldeações demoradas"
        *   *Check:* Identical. No error.

    *   **"estás sentado"**:
        *   BP: "você está sentado" (using *você* and *estar*)
        *   EP: "estás sentado" (using *tu* conjugation)
        *   *Check:* This is a necessary adaptation from the BP informal/standard *você* to the EP informal *tu* conjugation. This is acceptable and often preferred in EP when addressing the reader directly in a conversational style. No error.

    *   **"num autocarro"**:
        *   BP: "em um ônibus" (in a bus)
        *   EP: "num autocarro" (in a bus)
        *   *Check:*
            *   "ônibus" (BP) -> "autocarro" (EP). This is a standard, acceptable adaptation.
            *   "em um" (BP) -> "num" (EP contraction of *em um*). This is grammatically correct and natural in EP. No error.

    *   **"que vai para o teu bairro"**:
        *   BP: "que vai para o seu bairro" (using *seu*)
        *   EP: "que vai para o teu bairro" (using *teu*)
        *   *Check:* This maintains consistency with the shift from *você/seu* to *tu/teu*. No error.

5.  **Conclusion:** The candidate sentence successfully translates the meaning while making appropriate and natural adaptations from BP to standard EP (parada -> paragem, ônibus -> autocarro, você/seu -> tu/teu). There are no genuine errors based on the criteria.

thought
Thinking Process:

1.  **Analyze the Request:** The goal is to evaluate an EP candidate sentence against a BP source sentence, identifying specific errors based on four criteria (Meaning preservation, Required variety adaptation, Conservative rewriting, EP grammaticality/naturalness). I must use specific severity labels (`Minor`/`Major`) and output a strict JSON format.

2.  **Analyze the Source (BP):**
    *   *Source:* "E, sem uma parada sequer, nem baldeações demoradas, você está sentado em um ônibus que vai para o seu bairro."
    *   *Meaning:* And, without even a single stop, nor lengthy transfers, you are sitting in a bus that goes to your neighborhood.
    *   *Key elements:*
        *   "E" (And)
        *   "sem uma parada sequer" (without even a single stop)
        *   "nem baldeações demoradas" (nor lengthy transfers)
        *   "você está sentado" (you are sitting)
        *   "em um ônibus" (in a bus)
        *   "que vai para o seu bairro" (that goes to your neighborhood)

3.  **Analyze the Candidate (EP):**
    *   *Candidate:* "E, sem paragens ou trocas demoradas, estás sentado num autocarro que te leva até ao teu bairro."

4.  **Detailed Comparison and Error Check:**

    *   **"E, sem paragens ou trocas demoradas,"**
        *   BP: "sem uma parada sequer, nem baldeações demoradas"
        *   EP: "sem paragens ou trocas demoradas"
        *   *Check 1 (Meaning Preservation):*
            *   BP uses "parada sequer" (even a single stop), emphasizing the lack of *any* stop.
            *   EP uses "sem paragens" (without stops), which is fine, but loses the emphatic "sequer" (even/single).
            *   BP uses "nem baldeações demoradas" (nor lengthy transfers).
            *   EP uses "ou trocas demoradas" (or lengthy transfers).
            *   The shift from "nem" (nor) to "ou" (or) changes the logical relationship slightly, but the core meaning (lack of stops/transfers) is preserved.
            *   The loss of "sequer" is a slight loss of emphasis, but not a material change in fact.
        *   *Check 2 (Variety Adaptation/Grammar):* "paragens" (stops) and "trocas" (transfers) are correct EP. "ou" is acceptable.
        *   *Conclusion on this phrase:* The change from "sem uma parada sequer, nem baldeações demoradas" to "sem paragens ou trocas demoradas" is a slight simplification/loss of emphasis, but not a major error.

    *   **"estás sentado num autocarro"**
        *   BP: "você está sentado em um ônibus"
        *   EP: "estás sentado num autocarro"
        *   *Check 1 (Meaning Preservation):* Correct.
        *   *Check 2 (Variety Adaptation/Grammar):* BP uses *você* (standard BP pronoun/verb conjugation). EP uses *tu* (estás) and *autocarro* (standard EP vocabulary). This is a necessary and correct adaptation.
        *   *Check 3 (Conservative Rewriting):* None.

    *   **"que te leva até ao teu bairro."**
        *   BP: "que vai para o seu bairro."
        *   EP: "que te leva até ao teu bairro."
        *   *Check 1 (Meaning Preservation):* "vai para" (goes to) vs. "te leva até ao" (takes you to). This is a functional change (from simple movement to directed action), but the meaning is preserved.
        *   *Check 2 (Variety Adaptation/Grammar):* Correct EP usage.

5.  **Re-evaluating the Errors (Focusing on the most precise issues):**

    *   **Issue 1: "parada sequer" vs. "paragens"**
        *   The BP phrase "sem uma parada sequer" is highly emphatic. The EP candidate "sem paragens" is grammatically correct but loses this specific emphasis. However, since the goal is a *conservative* rewrite, and "sem paragens" is a perfectly acceptable way to convey the general idea, I should check if there is a more concrete error.

    *   **Issue 2: "nem baldeações demoradas" vs. "ou trocas demoradas"**
        *   The shift from "nem" (nor) to "ou" (or) is a change in logical structure.
        *   BP: (No stops) AND (No lengthy transfers).
        *   EP: (No stops) OR (No lengthy transfers). (This interpretation is slightly awkward, but the intent is clearly listing two things that are absent).
        *   If we interpret the BP structure as "without A, nor B," the EP structure should ideally maintain the negative conjunction.
        *   A more accurate translation of "sem A, nem B" would be "sem A nem B" or "nem A nem B."
        *   The use of "ou" here is a structural deviation from the source's negative coordination. This is a change in discourse function/logic.

6.  **Final Selection of Errors:**

    *   The most clear and identifiable error is the change in the negative coordination structure.
    *   BP: "sem uma parada sequer, nem baldeações demoradas" (A negative list)
    *   EP: "sem paragens ou trocas demoradas" (A disjunctive list)

    *   *Error Substring:* "ou trocas demoradas" (This captures the faulty conjunction and the resulting structure).
    *   *Severity:* This changes the logical relationship between the two conditions (stops and transfers), which materially compromises the faithful preservation of the source meaning. -> `Major`.

7.  **Construct JSON Output.** (Self-Correction: Ensure the substring is the shortest exact match for the problem.)

    *   Candidate: "E, sem paragens ou trocas demoradas, estás sentado num autocarro que te leva até ao teu bairro."
    *   Error: "ou trocas demoradas" (The conjunction 'ou' is the primary issue, but including 'trocas demoradas' makes the faulty construction clear). Let's use "ou trocas demoradas" as it represents the entire faulty phrase replacing "nem baldeações demoradas."

    *Wait, let's check if "ou" alone is sufficient.*
    If I use "ou", the error is just the conjunction.
    If I use "ou trocas demoradas", the error is the entire phrase.
    Since the error is the conjunction *and* the resulting structure, using the full phrase is clearer.

    Let's stick with "ou trocas demoradas".```json

            *   EP uses "ou trocas demoradas" (or lengthy transfers).
            *   The shift from "nem" (nor) to "ou" (or) changes the logical relationship slightly, but the core meaning (lack of stops/transfers) is preserved.
            *   The loss of "sequer" is a slight loss of emphasis, but not a material change in fact.
        *   *Check 2 (Variety Adaptation/Grammar):* "paragens" (stops) and "trocas" (transfers) are correct EP. "ou" is acceptable.
        *   *Conclusion on this phrase:* The change from "sem uma parada sequer, nem baldeações demoradas" to "sem paragens ou trocas demoradas" is a slight simplification/loss of emphasis, but not a major error.

    *   **"estás sentado num autocarro"**
        *   BP: "você está sentado em um ônibus"
        *   EP: "estás sentado num autocarro"
        *   *Check 1 (Meaning Preservation):* Correct.
        *   *Check 2 (Variety Adaptation/Grammar):* BP uses *você* (standard BP pronoun/verb conjugation). EP uses *tu* (estás) and *autocarro* (standard EP vocabulary). This is a necessary and correct adaptation.
        *   *Check 3 (Conservative Rewriting):* None.

    *   **"que te leva até ao teu bairro."**
        *   BP: "que vai para o seu bairro."
        *   EP: "que te leva até ao teu bairro."
        *   *Check 1 (Meaning Preservation):* "vai para" (goes to) vs. "te leva até ao" (takes you to). This is a functional change (from simple movement to directed action), but the meaning is preserved.
        *   *Check 2 (Variety Adaptation/Grammar):* Correct EP usage.

5.  **Re-evaluating the Errors (Focusing on the most precise issues):**

    *   **Issue 1: "parada sequer" vs. "paragens"**
        *   The BP phrase "sem uma parada sequer" is highly emphatic. The EP candidate "sem paragens" is grammatically correct but loses this specific emphasis. However, since the goal is a *conservative* rewrite, and "sem paragens" is a perfectly acceptable way to convey the general idea, I should check if there is a more concrete error.

    *   **Issue 2: "nem baldeações demoradas" vs. "ou trocas demoradas"**
        *   The shift from "nem" (nor) to "ou" (or) is a change in logical structure.
        *   BP: (No stops) AND (No lengthy transfers).
        *   EP: (No stops) OR (No lengthy transfers). (This interpretation is slightly awkward, but the intent is clearly listing two things that are absent).
        *   If we interpret the BP structure as "without A, nor B," the EP structure should ideally maintain the negative conjunction.
        *   A more accurate translation of "sem A, nem B" would be "sem A nem B" or "nem A nem B."
        *   The use of "ou" here is a structural deviation from the source's negative coordination. This is a change in discourse function/logic.

    *   The most clear and identifiable error is the change in the negative coordination structure.
    *   BP: "sem uma parada sequer, nem baldeações demoradas" (A negative list)
    *   EP: "sem paragens ou trocas demoradas" (A disjunctive list)

        *   BP: "parada" (acceptable in EP, but "paragem" is more common/standard in EP).
        *   EP: "paragem" (Correct adaptation/acceptable EP).

        *   BP: "nem baldeações demoradas"

            *   EP grammaticality/naturalness: Perfectly natural.
        *   *Decision:* No error.

    *   **"em um ônibus" (BP) vs. "num autocarro" (EP)**:
        *   BP: "ônibus" (common in BP, less common in EP).
        *   EP: "autocarro" (standard EP term for bus).
        *   BP: "em um" (preposition + article).
        *   EP: "num" (contraction of 'em um').
        *   *Status:* Correct adaptation/acceptable EP.

    *   **"que vai para o seu bairro"**:
        *   BP: "que vai para o seu bairro"
        *   EP: "que vai para o seu bairro"
        *   *Status:* OK.

thought
Thinking Process:

1.  **Analyze the Request:** The goal is to evaluate an EP candidate sentence against a BP source sentence, identifying specific errors based on four criteria (Meaning preservation, Required variety adaptation, Conservative rewriting, EP grammaticality/naturalness). I must use specific severity labels (`Minor`/`Major`) and output a strict JSON format.

2.  **Analyze the Source (BP):**
    *   *BP Source:* "O que vimos na destruição dos Budas de Bamiyan foi a redução da matéria, de uma substância sólida caindo e se desintegrando."
    *   *Meaning:* What we saw in the destruction of the Buddhas of Bamiyan was the reduction of matter, of a solid substance falling and disintegrating.
    *   *Grammar Check (BP):* "caindo e se desintegrando" uses gerunds, indicating simultaneous actions or manner.

3.  **Analyze the Candidate (EP):**
    *   *EP Candidate:* "O que vimos na destruição dos Budas de Bamiyan foi a redução da matéria, de uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se."
    *   *Change:* The gerund construction ("caindo e se desintegrando") was replaced by an infinitive construction ("a cair e desintegrar-se").

4.  **Compare and Evaluate Changes:**

    *   *Original structure:* [substância sólida] [caindo e se desintegrando]. (The substance was falling and disintegrating.)
    *   *Candidate structure:* [substância sólida] [a cair e desintegrar-se]. (The substance to fall and disintegrate.)

    *   **Meaning Preservation:** Does "a cair e desintegrar-se" preserve the meaning of "caindo e se desintegrando"?
        *   The gerund implies an ongoing process (it was falling/disintegrating).
        *   The infinitive construction "a + infinitive" often implies purpose, potential, or a description of the action that occurred (e.g., "a coisa a cair"). In this context, describing the state/action of the substance, the infinitive structure is grammatically possible but changes the nuance from a continuous action (gerund) to a description of the action itself (infinitive).
        *   However, in Portuguese, when describing the manner or state of a noun (substância sólida), both the gerund and the infinitive can sometimes be used, but the gerund is usually more natural for describing a process that *was* happening.
        *   Let's look closely at the construction: "de uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se." This structure is slightly awkward. If the intent is to describe the substance *in the process* of falling and disintegrating, the gerund is superior. If the intent is to describe the substance *as* something that falls and disintegrates, the infinitive might work, but the phrasing is less natural than the gerund.

    *   **EP Grammaticality and Naturalness:**
        *   "caindo e se desintegrando" (Gerunds) is perfectly natural and idiomatic for describing the process.
        *   "a cair e desintegrar-se" (Infinitive) is grammatically acceptable but sounds less natural and slightly formal/clunky compared to the gerund in this specific context of describing a physical process.
        *   *Self-Correction/Refinement:* Is it a *genuine error*? The infinitive structure is not strictly ungrammatical, but it is a less natural choice and deviates from the most idiomatic expression of the continuous action implied by the source.

    *   **Conservative Rewriting:** The change from gerund to infinitive is a structural change, not just a minor wording change. It alters the flow and naturalness.

5.  **Determine Severity:**
    *   The change is not a meaning error (the core idea remains).
    *   It is not a necessary adaptation (the gerund was already acceptable).
    *   It is an unnecessary change that reduces naturalness and idiomatic flow. This falls under "unnecessary changes to wording, grammar, structure, or register that were already acceptable in EP."

    *   *Conclusion:* This is a structural/stylistic issue that compromises naturalness. I classify this as a `Minor` error.

3.  **Analyze the Candidate (EP):**
    *   *EP Candidate:* "O que vimos na destruição dos Budas de Bamiyan foi a redução da matéria, de uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se."

    *   *Original structure:* [substância sólida] [caindo e se desintegrando]. (The substance was falling and disintegrating.)
    *   *Candidate structure:* [substância sólida] [a cair e desintegrar-se]. (The substance to fall and disintegrate.)

    *   **Meaning Preservation:** Does "a cair e desintegrar-se" preserve the meaning of "caindo e se desintegrando"?
        *   The gerund implies an ongoing process (it was falling/disintegrating).
        *   The infinitive construction "a + infinitive" often implies purpose, potential, or a description of the action that occurred (e.g., "a coisa a cair"). In this context, describing the state/action of the substance, the infinitive structure is grammatically possible but changes the nuance from a continuous action (gerund) to a description of the action itself (infinitive).
        *   However, in Portuguese, when describing the manner or state of a noun (substância sólida), both the gerund and the infinitive can sometimes be used, but the gerund is usually more natural for describing a process that *was* happening.
        *   Let's look closely at the construction: "de uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se." This structure is slightly awkward. If the intent is to describe the substance *in the process* of falling and disintegrating, the gerund is superior. If the intent is to describe the substance *as* something that falls and disintegrates, the infinitive might work, but the phrasing is less natural than the gerund.

        *   "caindo e se desintegrando" (Gerunds) is perfectly natural and idiomatic for describing the process.
        *   "a cair e desintegrar-se" (Infinitive) is grammatically acceptable but sounds less natural and slightly formal/clunky compared to the gerund in this specific context of describing a physical process.
        *   *Self-Correction/Refinement:* Is it a *genuine error*? The infinitive structure is not strictly ungrammatical, but it is a less natural choice and deviates from the most idiomatic expression of the continuous action implied by the source.

    *   *EP Candidate:* "O que vimos na destruição dos Budas de Bamiyan foi a redução da matéria, uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se."
    *   *Meaning:* What we saw in the destruction of the Buddhas of Bamiyan was the reduction of matter, a solid substance to fall and disintegrate.

4.  **Comparison and Error Check:**

    *   **Structure/Grammar:**
        *   BP uses the prepositional phrase structure: "de uma substância sólida caindo e se desintegrando." (Gerundial phrase modifying the substance).
        *   EP uses the infinitive structure: "uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se." (Infinitive phrase, often used with the particle 'a' to indicate manner or subsequent action).

    *   **Meaning Preservation:**
        *   The BP structure ("caindo e se desintegrando") describes the *process* that was observed (the substance *was* falling and disintegrating).
        *   The EP structure ("a cair e desintegrar-se") suggests a potential *future* action or a description of the substance's *potential* state, although in this context, it is often used to describe the action itself.
        *   However, the shift from the gerund (ongoing action in the past) to the infinitive (action description) changes the nuance slightly. The gerund is more natural for describing a continuous process observed in the past.
        *   *Self-Correction/Refinement:* While the infinitive construction is grammatically possible in EP, the gerund construction ("caindo e desintegrando-se") is the most direct and natural translation of the continuous action described by the BP gerunds. The candidate's use of the infinitive structure ("a cair e desintegrar-se") is slightly awkward and less natural than the original gerundial description.

    *   **Required Variety Adaptation / Conservative Rewriting / Naturalness:**
        *   The change from "de uma substância sólida caindo e se desintegrando" to "uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se" is a structural change.
        *   The use of the infinitive construction here is less idiomatic than the gerundial construction for describing a past, ongoing process.

    *   **Focusing on the specific change:**
        *   BP: "...de uma substância sólida caindo e se desintegrando."
        *   EP: "...uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se."
        *   The change from the gerund ("caindo e se desintegrando") to the infinitive construction ("a cair e desintegrar-se") is the primary issue. This makes the sentence less natural and slightly less precise regarding the observed process.

5.  **Severity Assessment:**
    *   The change is not a major meaning error (the core fact remains).
    *   It is a grammatical/idiomatic issue (less natural/precise usage of verb forms).
    *   It is a localized problem that does not substantially compromise the rewrite. -> `Minor`.

        *   The change from "de uma substância sólida caindo e se desintegrando" to "uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se" is a structural change.
        *   The use of the infinitive construction here is less idiomatic than the gerundial construction for describing a past, ongoing process.

    *   **Focusing on the specific change:**
        *   BP: "...de uma substância sólida caindo e se desintegrando."
        *   EP: "...uma substância sólida a cair e desintegrar-se."
        *   The change from the gerund ("caindo e se desintegrando") to the infinitive construction ("a cair e desintegrar-se") is the primary issue. This makes the sentence less natural and slightly less precise regarding the observed process.

5.  **Severity Assessment:**
    *   The change is not a major meaning error (the core fact remains).
    *   It is a grammatical/idiomatic issue (less natural/precise usage of verb forms).
    *   It is a localized problem that does not substantially compromise the rewrite. -> `Minor`.

    *   However, I should identify the specific part that is unnatural/incorrect compared to the source's intended continuous action. The shift to the infinitive structure is the issue.

        *   The EP structure ("a cair e desintegrar-se") suggests a potential *future* action or a description of the substance's *potential* state, although in this context, it is often used to describe the action itself.
        *   However, the shift from the gerund (ongoing action in the past) to the infinitive (action description) changes the nuance slightly. The gerund is more natural for describing a continuous process observed in the past.

        *   The use of the infinitive construction here is less idiomatic than the gerundial construction for describing a past, ongoing process.

thought
The user wants me to assign a holistic quality score (0-100) to an EP candidate translation based on provided context, source sentence, and validated error annotations.

**Context:**
BP Passage: [1] O menino abriu a geladeira.
BP Source Sentence: O menino abriu a geladeira.

**EP Candidate:**
O menino fechou o frigorífico.

**Validated Errors:**
`{"errors":[{"text":"fechou","severity":"Major"}]}`

**Analysis:**

1.  **Meaning Preservation:**
    *   BP Source: "O menino abriu a geladeira." (The boy opened the refrigerator.)
    *   EP Candidate: "O menino fechou o frigorífico." (The boy closed the refrigerator.)
    *   The verb changes from *abriu* (opened) to *fechou* (closed). This is a major semantic error.
    *   The noun changes from *geladeira* (common BP term) to *frigorífico* (standard EP term). This is a correct adaptation, but the verb error dominates.

2.  **Required BP→EP Adaptation:**
    *   *Geladeira* -> *Frigorífico* (Good adaptation).
    *   *Abriu* -> *Abriu* (If translated correctly).
    *   The candidate used *fechou*, which is incorrect for the source meaning.

3.  **Conservative Rewriting / EP Grammaticality/Naturalness:**
    *   Grammatically, "O menino fechou o frigorífico" is correct EP.
    *   However, the meaning is fundamentally wrong.

4.  **Scoring Criteria:**
    *   The error annotation explicitly marks "fechou" as a "Major" error.
    *   A major semantic error (opening vs. closing) significantly compromises meaning preservation.
    *   Since the error array is not empty, the score must be below 100.

**Conclusion:**
The candidate successfully adapted the vocabulary (*geladeira* -> *frigorífico*), but the core action (opening vs. closing) is reversed. This is a major failure in meaning preservation.

*   85–100: Very good (No major errors)
*   65–80: Good (Minor errors, good flow)
*   45–60: Acceptable (Some errors, but meaning is mostly preserved)
*   25–40: Borderline (Significant errors, meaning is compromised)

   * EP: Ela está a falar com o Pedro. (Present continuous, action in progress - using the gerund construction common in EP)
   * Meaning is preserved.

2. **Required variety adaptation (BP -> EP):**
   * BP uses *estar + gerúndio* (está falando).
   * EP often prefers *estar + a + infinitivo* (está a falar) for continuous actions.
   * This adaptation is necessary and correct for standard EP.

3. **Conservative rewriting:**
   * The change from *falando* to *a falar* is a standard, natural adaptation, not an unnecessary rewrite.

4. **EP grammaticality and naturalness:**
   * "Ela está a falar com o Pedro" is perfectly grammatical and natural in standard European Portuguese.

**Conclusion:** The candidate sentence is a correct and natural adaptation of the BP source into standard EP. There are no errors.

    *   Meaning: She is talking to Maria.

        *   Source Subject: Ela (She) -> Candidate Subject: Ela (She). (OK)

        *   BP "está falando" -> EP "está a falar". This is a correct and necessary adaptation (gerund usage). (OK)

        *   The change from Pedro to Maria is a factual change, not just a stylistic one.

    *   **EP Grammaticality and Naturalness:**
        *   "Ela está a falar com a Maria." is grammatically correct EP.

    *   Severity: `Major`.

The user wants me to assign a holistic quality score (0-100) to an EP candidate translation based on several criteria: meaning preservation, required BP→EP adaptation, conservative rewriting, EP grammaticality, naturalness, context, and register.

I must treat the provided Stage 1 error annotations as fixed evidence.

**Context:**
BP Passage: [1] Ela está falando com o Pedro.
BP Source Sentence: Ela está falando com o Pedro.
EP Candidate: Ela está a falar com a Maria.

**Validated Stage 1 annotations:**
`{"errors":[{"text":"a Maria","severity":"Major"}]}`

**Analysis:**

1.  **Meaning Preservation:**
    *   BP Source: "Ela está falando com o Pedro." (She is talking to Pedro.)
    *   EP Candidate: "Ela está a falar com a Maria." (She is talking to Maria.)
    *   The core structure (She is talking to X) is preserved, but the name/person (Pedro -> Maria) has been changed. This is a major meaning deviation/error in translation, even if the grammar is correct.

2.  **Required BP→EP Adaptation:**
    *   BP uses the gerund structure: "está falando" (is talking).
    *   EP Candidate uses the periphrastic structure: "está a falar" (is talking). This is a correct and natural adaptation from BP to EP.
    *   The article change (o Pedro -> a Maria) is also a necessary adaptation if the name changes, but the name change itself is the issue.

3.  **Conservative Rewriting:**
    *   The candidate changed "Pedro" to "Maria." This is not conservative rewriting; it is a substitution of a key entity.

4.  **EP Grammaticality, Naturalness, Context, and Register:**
    *   Grammatically, "Ela está a falar com a Maria" is perfectly correct and natural EP.
    *   However, the change in the name/person violates meaning preservation, which is a critical failure.

5.  **Scoring based on constraints:**
    *   The `errors` array is *not* empty. Therefore, the score must be below 100.
    *   The error is classified as "Major" (changing the recipient of the action).
    *   While the Portuguese is flawless, the failure to preserve meaning is severe.

**Conclusion:**
The candidate successfully handles the BP→EP linguistic shift (gerund to periphrastic construction) but fails catastrophically on meaning preservation by changing the name of the person involved (Pedro to Maria). Since the error is Major and affects the core meaning, the score must be low, despite the high quality of the Portuguese itself.

*   85–100: Very good (No major errors)
*   65–80: Good (Minor errors, good overall quality)
*   45–60: Acceptable (Noticeable errors, but meaning is mostly preserved)
*   25–40: Borderline (Significant errors, meaning compromised)
*   0–20: Not acceptable (Unintelligible or completely wrong)

   BP: "Ela está falando com o Pedro." (She is talking to Pedro.)
   EP Candidate: "Ela está a falar." (She is talking.)
   The candidate dropped the object ("com o Pedro"). This is a loss of meaning/information, which is a major issue.

2. **BP→EP Adaptation:**
   BP uses the gerund structure "estar + gerundio" ("está falando").
   EP typically uses the periphrastic structure "estar + a + infinitive" ("está a falar").
   The candidate correctly applied the EP structure ("está a falar").

*   Since the candidate is "Ela está a falar," it is grammatically fine but semantically incomplete.

Thinking Process:

1.  **Analyze the Request:** The goal is to evaluate an EP candidate sentence against a BP source sentence, identifying specific errors based on four criteria (Meaning preservation, Required variety adaptation, Conservative rewriting, EP grammaticality/naturalness). I must use specific severity labels (`Minor`, `Major`) and output a strict JSON format.

2.  **Analyze the Source (BP):**
    *   BP Source: "Ela está falando com a Maria."
    *   Meaning: She is talking to Maria.

3.  **Analyze the Candidate (EP):**
    *   EP Candidate: "Ela está a falar com o Pedro."
    *   Meaning: She is talking to Pedro.

4.  **Compare Source vs. Candidate (Error Check):**

    *   **Meaning Preservation Check:**
        *   Source Subject: Ela (She) -> Candidate Subject: Ela (She). (OK)
        *   Source Verb/Tense: está falando (is talking - BP gerund) -> Candidate Verb/Tense: está a falar (is talking - EP progressive). (OK, this is a necessary adaptation).
        *   Source Object/Recipient: a Maria (Maria - feminine) -> Candidate Object/Recipient: o Pedro (Pedro - masculine). (ERROR: Change in entity/person).

    *   **Required Variety Adaptation Check:**
        *   BP "está falando" -> EP "está a falar". This is a necessary and correct adaptation. (OK)

    *   **Conservative Rewriting Check:**
        *   The change from Maria to Pedro is a factual change, not just a stylistic one.

    *   **EP Grammaticality/Naturalness Check:**
        *   "Ela está a falar com o Pedro" is grammatically correct EP.

5.  **Identify the Error:**
    *   The core error is the change of the recipient from "Maria" (feminine) to "Pedro" (masculine). This is a change in fact/entity, violating Meaning Preservation.

6.  **Determine Severity:**
    *   Changing the person involved in the action (Maria to Pedro) materially compromises the meaning of the rewrite. This is a `Major` error.

7.  **Extract the Substring:**
    *   The change occurs in the object phrase: "a Maria" vs "o Pedro".
    *   The candidate uses "o Pedro". This is the problematic part that differs from the source.

8.  **Final JSON Construction:**
    *   Error: "o Pedro"
    *   Severity: Major

9.  **Review Constraints:**
    *   Strict JSON output.
    *   No reasoning/markdown.
    *   Use shortest exact substring. (Checked: "o Pedro").

thought
The user wants me to assign a holistic quality score (0-100) to an EP candidate translation based on provided context, source, and validated error annotations.

**Context:**
BP Passage: [1] Ela está falando com a Maria.
BP Source Sentence: Ela está falando com a Maria.
EP Candidate: Ela está a falar com o Pedro.
Validated Errors: `{"errors":[{"text":"o Pedro","severity":"Major"}]}`

**Evaluation Criteria:**
1. Meaning preservation.
2. Required BP→EP adaptation.
3. Conservative rewriting.
4. EP grammaticality, naturalness, context, and register.

**Constraint Checklist & Confidence Score:**
1. Assign holistic 0-100 score: Yes.
2. Use provided bands: Yes.
3. Treat annotations as fixed evidence: Yes.
4. Do not add/remove/merge/reinterpret/reclassify errors: Yes.
5. Score based on meaning, adaptation, conservatism, grammar/naturalness/context/register: Yes.
6. Use intermediate scores: Yes.
7. Do not use mechanical points-per-error formula: Yes.
8. Major errors must result in score < 100: Yes.
9. Return exactly one JSON object: Yes.
10. Return only valid JSON: Yes.

**Analysis:**

1. **Meaning Preservation:**
   * BP Source: Ela está falando com a Maria. (She is talking to Maria.)
   * EP Candidate: Ela está a falar com o Pedro. (She is talking to Pedro.)
   * The meaning is fundamentally altered because the name "Maria" (the recipient) was changed to "Pedro." This is a major failure in meaning preservation.

2. **BP→EP Adaptation:**
   * BP: "está falando" (Gerund usage common in BP)
   * EP Candidate: "está a falar" (Correct EP structure for continuous action). This adaptation is correct.
   * BP: "com a Maria"
   * EP Candidate: "com o Pedro"
   * The gender/name change is the issue, not the grammatical structure.

3. **Conservative Rewriting:**
   * The change from Maria (female) to Pedro (male) is not conservative.

4. **EP Grammaticality/Naturalness:**
   * "Ela está a falar com o Pedro" is grammatically correct and natural EP.

5. **Error Impact:**
   * The validated error is `{"text":"o Pedro","severity":"Major"}`. This confirms the name change is a critical error.
   * Since the core meaning (who she is talking to) is wrong, the score must be low.

**Scoring Justification:**
The candidate successfully handles the tense/aspect shift (está falando -> está a falar), which is a required BP->EP adaptation. However, the substitution of the recipient's name (Maria -> Pedro) is a major semantic error. This error fundamentally breaks meaning preservation.

A score in the 25-40 range (Borderline) seems appropriate. It is not completely unusable (it is grammatically correct EP), but the major semantic error prevents it from being acceptable (45-60).

Let's aim for the lower end of the Borderline band, perhaps 30-35.

The user wants me to assign a holistic quality score (0-100) to an EP candidate rewrite based on several criteria: meaning preservation, required BP→EP adaptation, conservative rewriting, EP grammaticality, naturalness, context, and register.

The input data is:
1.  **BP Context:** [1] Comprei um celular ontem.
2.  **BP Source Sentence:** Comprei um celular ontem.
3.  **EP Candidate:** Comprei um computador ontem.
4.  **Validated Errors:** `{"errors":[{"text":"computador","severity":"Major"}]}`

**Analysis:**

1.  **Meaning Preservation:**
    *   BP Source: "Comprei um celular ontem." (I bought a cell phone yesterday.)
    *   EP Candidate: "Comprei um computador ontem." (I bought a computer yesterday.)
    *   The meaning is significantly altered. "Celular" (cell phone/mobile phone) is replaced by "computador" (computer). This is a major semantic error.

2.  **Required BP→EP Adaptation:**
    *   The original sentence is already very standard Portuguese (BP/EP). The only potential adaptation would be stylistic, but the core structure is fine.
    *   The candidate made a lexical substitution that changes the meaning, which is not an adaptation but an error.

3.  **Conservative Rewriting:**
    *   The rewrite is not conservative because it changes the object ("celular" -> "computador").

4.  **EP Grammaticality/Naturalness/Context/Register:**
    *   Grammatically, "Comprei um computador ontem" is perfectly correct EP.
    *   However, the context requires the object to be a mobile phone ("celular").

5.  **Error Assessment:**
    *   The validated error is `{"text":"computador","severity":"Major"}`.
    *   Since there is a Major error, the score must be below 100.

**Scoring:**

The candidate is grammatically correct but fails fundamentally on meaning preservation due to a major lexical substitution. This is a critical failure in translation/rewriting quality.

*   85–100: Very good (No major errors)
*   65–80: Good (Minor issues, good flow)
*   45–60: Acceptable (Some noticeable errors, but meaning is mostly preserved)
*   25–40: Borderline (Significant errors, meaning compromised)
*   0–20: Not acceptable (Unintelligible or completely wrong)

Replacing "celular" with "computador" changes the core subject of the sentence. This is a major semantic error, placing it firmly in the "Borderline" or potentially lower range, as the translation is fundamentally incorrect relative to the source meaning.

I will assign a score in the 25-40 range. 30 seems appropriate, reflecting that the structure is perfect, but the content is wrong.

**Final Check:**
Error exists -> Score < 100.
Major semantic error -> Low score.

    *   Meaning: I bought a mobile phone yesterday.

        *   *Action:* Source is "Vendi" (I sold). Candidate is "Comprei" (I bought). -> **Change in action/fact.** (Major)

        *   *Time:* Source is "hoje" (today). Candidate is "ontem" (yesterday). -> **Change in time expression.** (Major)

    *   **Criteria Check:**
        *   *Meaning preservation:* Failed (Action and Time changed).
        *   *Required variety adaptation:* Not applicable (The changes are factual/semantic, not just dialectal).
        *   *Conservative rewriting:* Not applicable (The changes are fundamental).
        *   *EP grammaticality and naturalness:* The candidate is grammatically correct EP, but semantically incorrect relative to the source.

        *   Substring: "Comprei"

    *   EP Candidate: "Ele perdeu o autocarro cedo." (He *missed* the bus early.) -> Action: Missing.
    *   The candidate fundamentally changes the meaning from "caught" to "missed." This is a major semantic error.

    *   "cedo" (early) -> "cedo" (early). (Correct.)

    *   Grammatically, "Ele perdeu o autocarro cedo" is correct EP.
    *   However, the change in meaning is not conservative.

4.  **Scoring:**
    *   The annotation explicitly marks "perdeu" as a Major error.
    *   The error changes the core meaning of the sentence (caught vs. missed).
    *   A major semantic error prevents the score from being high.

*   65–80: Good
*   45–60: Acceptable
*   25–40: Borderline
*   0–20: Not acceptable

    *   *esperando* (waiting - gerund): BP uses *estou esperando*. EP often prefers *estar à espera* or *esperar*.
    *   *à espera* (waiting for): This is a standard, correct EP construction for "waiting for."
    *   *o trem* (the train): BP uses *o trem*. EP uses *o comboio*. This is a necessary vocabulary adaptation (BP -> EP).
    *   *do comboio* (of the train): Correct usage with *à espera*.

    *   **Meaning Preservation:** The meaning ("I am waiting for the train") is perfectly preserved.
    *   **Required Variety Adaptation:**
        *   *trem* -> *comboio*: Necessary adaptation. (Correctly done).
        *   *estou esperando* -> *estou à espera*: This is a valid and common EP adaptation, although *estou a esperar* is also possible. *Estou à espera* is perfectly acceptable EP.
    *   **Conservative Rewriting:** The change from *estou esperando* to *estou à espera* is a structural change, but it is a natural and conservative way to express the continuous action of waiting in EP, especially when using the prepositional phrase *à espera*.
    *   **EP Grammaticality and Naturalness:** The candidate sentence "Eu estou à espera do comboio" is grammatically correct and natural in standard EP.

2.  **Analyze the Input Data:**
    *   **BP Context:** [1] Eu estou esperando o trem. (I am waiting for the train.)
    *   **BP Source Sentence:** Eu estou esperando o trem.
    *   **EP Candidate:** Eu estou à espera do autocarro.
    *   **Validated Errors (Fixed Evidence):** `{"errors":[{"text":"do autocarro","severity":"Major"}]}`
3.  **Evaluate the Candidate against the Errors:**
    *   The error annotation targets `"do autocarro"`.
    *   *Original BP:* "o trem" (the train).
    *   *Candidate EP:* "do autocarro" (of the bus/coach).
    *   *Error Analysis:*
        *   **Meaning Preservation:** The candidate changed "trem" (train) to "autocarro" (bus/coach). This is a semantic error (meaning change).
        *   **Grammar/Style (BP->EP):**
            *   "Eu estou esperando" (BP usage, often used for continuous action) -> "Eu estou à espera" (EP standard way of saying "I am waiting for"). This adaptation is generally good.
            *   "o trem" (BP) -> "o autocarro" (EP equivalent for bus/coach).
            *   The prepositional phrase structure "estar à espera de X" is correct EP usage.
            *   The error is specifically the substitution of the object ("trem" -> "autocarro") and the resulting contraction ("o trem" -> "do autocarro").
4.  **Determine the Impact of the Error:**
    *   The error is classified as "Major."
    *   It fundamentally changes the meaning of the sentence (Train vs. Bus).
    *   Since a major semantic error exists, the score must be significantly penalized.
5.  **Assess Overall Quality (Ignoring the Major Error for a moment):**
    *   *Adaptation:* "Eu estou esperando" -> "Eu estou à espera" (Good EP adaptation).
    *   *Grammar:* "Eu estou à espera de..." (Grammatically correct EP structure).
    *   *Naturalness:* The structure is natural EP.
6.  **Synthesize the Score:**
    *   The structure and register are good (65-80 range).
    *   However, the core meaning is corrupted by replacing "trem" with "autocarro." This is a critical failure in meaning preservation.
    *   A major semantic error usually pulls the score out of the "Good" band and into the "Acceptable" or "Borderline" band, unless the rest of the sentence is perfect.
    *   The adaptation itself is strong, but the substitution is fatal.
    *   *Target Range:* 45-60 (Acceptable) or 25-40 (Borderline).
    *   Given that the grammatical structure is sound, but the meaning is wrong, a score in the mid-40s seems appropriate. It is understandable, but incorrect.

    *   The omission is the entire phrase "o trem."

    *   **EP grammaticality and naturalness:** "Eu estou à espera" is grammatically correct EP, but it fails the meaning preservation test.

        *   Source: "Eu estou esperando o ônibus." (I am waiting for the bus.)
        *   Candidate: "Eu estou à espera do comboio." (I am waiting for the train/tram/bus, depending on regional usage, but 'comboio' is often used for train/tram in EP.)
        *   *Change:* "ônibus" (bus) -> "comboio" (train/tram/bus). While 'comboio' can sometimes refer to a bus, it is a semantic shift from the specific 'ônibus' in the source. This is a change in entity/fact.
        *   *Action:* The core meaning ("I am waiting") is preserved, but the specific object ("bus") is changed to a potentially different object ("comboio"). This is a change in fact/entity.

    *   **Required Variety Adaptation (BP -> EP):**
        *   BP: "estou esperando" (Gerund usage, common in BP).
        *   EP Candidate: "estou à espera" (Standard EP construction for "waiting for"). This is a correct and necessary adaptation for standard EP.
        *   BP: "ônibus" -> EP: "comboio". This is a lexical adaptation, but the semantic shift noted above is the primary issue.

    *   **Conservative Rewriting:**
        *   The change from "estou esperando" to "estou à espera" is a necessary stylistic/grammatical adaptation, not an unnecessary rewrite.

    *   **EP Grammaticality and Naturalness:**
        *   "Eu estou à espera do comboio." is grammatically correct and natural in EP.

4.  **Identify Errors:**

    *   The primary error is the substitution of "ônibus" (bus) with "comboio" (train/tram/bus). This constitutes a change in the entity/fact being waited for.

5.  **Determine Severity:**
    *   Changing the specific object (bus vs. train/tram) materially compromises the meaning preservation requirement, even if the general action is correct. This is a `Major` error.

6.  **Formulate the Output:**
    *   Error substring: "do comboio" (or just "comboio" if we focus on the noun change, but since the whole phrase is different, let's focus on the noun substitution).
    *   Let's use "comboio" as the specific change that replaces "ônibus".
